│ **Deviation:** Use custom SolverCheckpointSaver instead of                   │
│ langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.PostgresSaver                                  │
│ **Document:** docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md                           │
│ **Reason:** Installed langgraph-checkpoint-postgres v3.0.2 expects           │
│ columns/tables absent from 001_initial_schema.py (checkpoint_ns,             │
│ checkpoint_blobs, checkpoint_writes.type, checkpoint_writes.task_path, BYTEA │
│ storage); migrations are out of scope for P2.                                │
│ **Approved by:** Architect                                                   │
│ **Incorporated into spec:** docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md updated to  │
│ reference SolverCheckpointSaver and Gate D test changes.                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ## 2026-01-03 - P3.3                                                         │
│ **Deviation:** Clear human_decision after routing instead of in              │
│ process_decision_node                                                        │
│ **Document:** docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md §8.4                      │
│ **Reason:** Doc 3 §8.4 clears human_decision in process_decision_node, but   │
│ §8.2 route_after_decision reads it AFTER that node runs. Clearing before     │
│ routing causes graph to default to "end" instead of correct branch.          │
│ Resolution: clear in advance_node (approve path), structure_node (reject     │
│ path), and validate_node (modify path).                                      │
│ **Approved by:** Co-Developer review + plan approval                         │
│ **Incorporated into spec:** Updated docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md     │
│ §8.4 process_decision_node and advance_node.                                 │

│ ### 13.3 Status and Phase Mapping                                            │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ Status (External)              Phase (Internal)                              │
│ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────                            │
│ not_started                    (n/a)                                         │
│ in_progress                    received, analyzing, structuring, validating  │
│ awaiting_clarification         eliciting                                     │
│ awaiting_review                reviewing                                     │
│ approved                       complete                                      │
│ revision_requested             (transitions to in_progress)                  │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### 13.4 Phase Flow                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ RECEIVED → ANALYZING → [ELICITING] → STRUCTURING → VALIDATING → REVIEWING →  │
│ COMPLETE                                                                     │
│                            ↑                            │                    │
│                            └────────────────────────────┘ (validation        │
│ failure)                                                                     │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### 13.5 Document Versions                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ V1: Initial creation (forward dependencies only)                             │
│ V2: First refinement (cross-document feedback)                               │
│ V3: Final refinement (authoritative for Pass 2)                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### 13.6 Pass 1 Gate Policies                                                │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ none:        No gates, fully autonomous                                      │
│ per_step:    Gate after each step's V3 (default interactive)                 │
│ end_of_pass: Gate only after all 10 steps complete                           │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### 13.7 Instance Types                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │

│ Instance N:  Domain          "Practice"       Substantive knowledge          │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### 13.8 API Endpoints Summary                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ POST   /workflow                    Create new workflow                      │
│ GET    /workflow/{id}               Get workflow state                       │
│ POST   /workflow/{id}/clarify       Submit clarification answers             │
│ POST   /workflow/{id}/resume        Submit human decision                    │
│ (approve/reject/modify)                                                      │

│ ```                                                                          │

│ **Test Code:**                                                               │
│ ```python                                                                    │
│ async def test_audit_trail_complete():                                       │
│     # Complete full workflow                                                 │
│     workflow = await create_and_complete_workflow()                          │
│                                                                              │
│     # Query audit log                                                        │
│     audit_entries = await get_audit_log(workflow.workflow_id)                │
│                                                                              │
│     # Verify completeness                                                    │
│     assert len(audit_entries) >= 12  # At least 4 transitions × 3 steps      │
│                                                                              │
│     # Verify ordering                                                        │
│     timestamps = [e.created_at for e in audit_entries]                       │
│     assert timestamps == sorted(timestamps)                                  │
│                                                                              │
│     # Verify all steps represented                                           │
│     steps = set(e.step_name for e in audit_entries if e.step_name)           │
│     assert StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION in steps                              │
│     assert StepName.REQUIREMENTS in steps                                    │
│     assert StepName.OBJECTIVES in steps                                      │
│                                                                              │
│     # Verify terminal states                                                 │
│     approved_events = [e for e in audit_entries if e.to_status ==            │
│ 'approved']                                                                  │

│ ### 9.3 SSE Event Types                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ The SSE stream (IP-002) delivers typed events to the frontend:               │
│                                                                              │
│ | Event Type | Purpose | Payload | Frontend Action |                         │
│ |------------|---------|---------|-----------------|                         │
│ | `text_delta` | LLM token streaming | `{ "delta": "..." }` | Append to      │
│ response display |                                                           │
│ | `status_update` | Workflow state change | `{ "status": "...", "phase":     │
│ "...", "step": N }` | Update progress indicator |                            │
│ | `interrupt` | Gate activation | `{ "type": "clarification" \| "review",    │
│ "payload": {...} }` | Show gate UI |                                         │
│ | `error` | Error occurred | `{ "code": "...", "message": "..." }` | Display │
│ error |                                                                      │
│ | `complete` | Step/workflow complete | `{ "artifact_id": "..." }` | Show    │
│ completion, enable next action |                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ **SSE Message Format:**                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ event: text_delta                                                            │
│ data: {"delta": "The system shall"}                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ event: text_delta                                                            │
│ data: {"delta": " provide..."}                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ --                                                                           │
│ ### 13.9 SSE Event Types                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ text_delta:     LLM token streaming                                          │
│ status_update:  Workflow state change                                        │
│ interrupt:      Gate activation (clarification or review)                    │
│ error:          Error occurred                                               │
│ complete:       Step or workflow complete                                    │
│ ```                                                                          │

✦ Option 1: Log deviation in DECISIONS.md (Recommended)

> > My view: this is a spec correction, not a deviation. Doc 0’s deviation log
  is
    for cases where code must intentionally violate an authoritative spec. Here
  we
    can (and should) align the spec itself to the repo’s authoritative layout
    (apps/api/...), so there’s no enduring violation to log.
  An enduring violation is when we knowingly implement behavior or structure
    that continues to differ from an authoritative spec, because changing the
  spec
    or code is impractical or out of scope. That decision needs to be logged in
    docs/DECISIONS.md.

    Examples:

    - Checkpoint saver: Using SolverCheckpointSaver instead of
      langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.PostgresSaver due to schema mismatch and
      migration scope — logged in docs/DECISIONS.md (already done).
    - API contract mismatch: If we kept text_delta/status_update events in code
      for backward compatibility while Doc 3 specifies artifact.delta/step.*,
  and
      chose not to change either, that persistent mismatch should be logged.
    - Schema divergence: If we intentionally omit or rename fields in packages/
      contracts/*.schema.json because a downstream system can’t handle them, and
      we keep that difference, it should be logged.

    In contrast, if we can align the specs themselves (like fixing backend/ →
    apps/api/), that’s a correction, not a deviation.

│ # EXECUTION PROTOCOL                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Starting a New Problem                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ 1. Identify the instance level needed:                                       │
│    - Is Instance 0 (abstract methodology) defined? If not, create it.        │
│    - Is Instance 1 (software implementation) defined? If not, create it.     │
│    - Then create Instance N for your specific domain.                        │
│                                                                              │
│ 2. For each step (1, 2, 3, ...):                                             │
│                                                                              │
│    a. Pass 1: Create/refine methodology                                      │
│       - Create V1: Data Sheet → To Do List → Guidance → Detailed Procedure   │
│       - Create V2: Refine each document considering the others               │
│       - Create V3: Final refinement following same pattern                   │
│       - Ensure V3 completeness (no ellipses, no placeholders)                │
│       - (Runs autonomously)                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│    b. Pass 2: Execute methodology                                            │
│       - Follow V3 Detailed Procedure                                         │
│       - Produce step output                                                  │
│       - Present to human for review                                          │
│       - Request explicit approval before proceeding                          │
│       - (Do not proceed without explicit approval)                           │
│                                                                              │
│ 3. After Step 3 approved:                                                    │
│    - Problem Statement + Requirements + Objectives = definition complete     │
│    - Can attempt first draft solution, or proceed through Steps 4-7 for      │
│ verification                                                                 │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Human Interaction Points                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ | Point | Type | Protocol |                                                  │
│ |-------|------|----------|                                                  │
│ | Pass 1 | Interrupt (optional) | Human may stop autonomous execution |      │
│ | Pass 2 end | Gate (required) | Must wait for explicit approval |           │
│ | Unclear input | Elicitation | Ask clarifying questions per Clarification   │
│ Policy |                                                                     │
│ | Validation failure | Escalation | Present issue, ask for guidance |        │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Checkpointing                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ - Save state after each phase completion                                     │
│ - Save state after each pass completion                                      │
│ - Enable rollback to any checkpoint                                          │
│ - Maintain full traceability                                                 │
│                                                                              │

│ # PRINCIPLES                                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ 1. **Methodology before execution** - Know HOW before doing WHAT             │
│ 2. **Iteration improves quality** - V1→V2→V3 catches gaps                    │

> evaluate the approach of Co-Developer-1.  Scrutinize the decisions made and
  compare with your directives above.  Reflect on the decisions made and give
  your perspective on what you think is best to achieve the objective.

  <Co-Developer-1>
  Disagree with the revised proposal.

    Reasons:

    - It still relies on ChatOpenAI (LangChain chat interface), which does not
      guarantee the Responses API call shape you explicitly required. It hides
      request/response schema, so we can’t prove compliance.
    - It returns a chat model object, not a normalized Responses payload; P5.1
      needs an adapter that calls /v1/responses directly and parses the response
      into a stable LLMResponse.
    - The plan omits how to parse output_text / output[].content[] from
  Responses,
      which is the core of the “call shape” requirement.

    Recommended direction instead:

    - Implement OpenAIResponsesAdapter using httpx.AsyncClient to POST to
  https://
      api.openai.com/v1/responses with model="gpt-5.2" and input=[{role,
      content}, ...].
    - Hard‑fail if openai_api_key is missing.
    - Normalize response into LLMResponse(content, provider, model, usage) by
      reading output_text if present, else extracting the first
  output[].content[]
      text segment.
    - Update apps/api/config.py default openai_model = "gpt-5.2" and update the
      config snippet in docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md.

  </Co-Developer-1>

  Responding with gemini-3-pro-preview
✦ I have reviewed Co-Developer-1's critique and recommendations.

  Evaluation:

  Co-Developer-1 is correct. My previous proposal relied on ChatOpenAI from
  LangChain, which wraps the standard Chat Completions API
  (/v1/chat/completions). Your explicit instruction was to use the "OpenAI
  Responses API call shape", which implies a distinct endpoint or payload
  structure (likely the newer /v1/responses endpoint mentioned in the provided
  context from projects/chirality-framework/GEMINI.md, even if hypothetical or
  specific to this environment). LangChain's standard ChatOpenAI does not target
  this specific API shape by default.

  To rigorously satisfy the requirement "specify exclusively the OPENAI
  Responses API call shape", we must build a direct adapter using httpx rather
  than relying on a generic LangChain wrapper that might mask the specific API
  contract.

  Reflected Decision:

  I will adopt Co-Developer-1's recommendation. We will build a custom adapter
  OpenAIResponsesAdapter that makes raw HTTP requests to ensure exact compliance
  with the requested call shape.

  Revised Plan (Aligned with Co-Developer-1):

   1. Modify `apps/api/config.py`:
       * Update openai_model default to "gpt-5.2".

   2. Modify `apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py`:
       * Remove: LangChain dependencies for this adapter.
       * Implement: OpenAIResponsesAdapter class.
       * Method: generate(messages: list[dict]) -> LLMResponse.
       * Implementation:
           * Use httpx.AsyncClient.
           * POST to /v1/responses (relative to base URL).
           * Payload: {"model": "gpt-5.2", "input": messages} (adhering to the
             "Responses" shape where input is the conversation history).
           * Response Parsing: Handle the specific shape of the Responses API
             (e.g., looking for output_text or output list).
           * Return: A normalized LLMResponse dataclass.

   3. Create `apps/api/domain/llm.py`:
       * Define the LLMResponse dataclass (content, usage, etc.) to decouple
         domain from the specific adapter response format.

   4. Create `apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py`:
       * Mock httpx.AsyncClient.post.
       * Verify the request payload has model="gpt-5.2" and input (not messages
         like Chat Completions).
       * Verify response parsing.

  This approach ensures strict compliance with the "Responses API call shape"
  requirement.

  I am ready to instruct the Senior Developer to proceed with this plan.

  Objective: Implement a custom infrastructure adapter that exclusively uses the
  OpenAI Responses API with the gpt-5.2 model, bypassing generic LangChain
  wrappers to ensure exact adherence to the specified call shape.

  Negative Patterns to Avoid:
   * Do NOT use `langchain_openai.ChatOpenAI`: It targets /v1/chat/completions,
     violating the "Responses API" requirement.
   * Do NOT use generic model names: Hardcode gpt-5.2 as the default in config,
     ensuring the adapter uses it.
   * Do NOT leak HTTP details to Domain: The adapter must return a clean domain
     object (LLMResponse), not a raw httpx.Response or dictionary.
   * Do NOT mix provider logic: For this slice, strictly implement the OpenAI
     Responses path. Do not clutter code with stubs for Anthropic/Google yet.

  Files to Create/Modify:

   1. `apps/api/config.py`
       * Action: Update default openai_model to "gpt-5.2".

   2. `apps/api/domain/llm.py` (New File)
       * Action: Define domain interfaces.
       * Content:

   1         from dataclasses import dataclass
   2         from typing import List, Dict, Optional
   3 
   4         @dataclass
   5         class LLMResponse:
   6             content: str
   7             role: str = "assistant"
   8             model_used: str = ""
   9             usage: Optional[Dict[str, int]] = None

   3. `apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py`
       * Action: Implement custom adapter.
       * Imports: httpx, apps.api.config, apps.api.domain.llm.
       * Class: OpenAIResponsesAdapter.
       * Methods:
           * __init__(self, api_key: str, model: str = "gpt-5.2"): Validate key
             presence.
           * async def generate(self, messages: List[Dict[str, str]]) ->
             LLMResponse:
               * Construct URL: https://api.openai.com/v1/responses.
               * Construct Payload: {"model": self.model, "input": messages}
                 (Note: key is input, not messages).
               * Execute: httpx.AsyncClient.post with Bearer auth.
               * Parse: Extract content from output_text (primary) or
                 output[0].content (fallback).
               * Return: LLMResponse.

   4. `apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py` (New File)
       * Action: Verify specific call shape.
       * Tests:
           * test_adapter_uses_responses_endpoint: Mock httpx and assert URL
             ends in /v1/responses.
           * test_adapter_payload_shape: Assert payload uses input key and
             gpt-5.2 model.
           * test_response_parsing: Mock a successful JSON response and verify
             LLMResponse fields.

  Verification Command:
  pytest apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py

  <Co-Developer-1>
  Comprehensive plan — P5.1 LLM Adapter (OpenAI Responses API, gpt‑5.2)

    Objective

    - Deliver a strict OpenAI Responses API adapter (non‑streaming) that can be
      consumed by later P5.2 prompt work, with hard‑failure on missing API key
  and
      spec‑aligned configuration.

    Scope

    - In scope: adapter implementation, minimal factory, config default update,
      spec snippet update, optional unit tests.
    - Out of scope: multi‑provider support, prompts, node integration,
  streaming,
      observability.

    Files to create/modify

    - apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py (replace stub with implementation)
    - apps/api/config.py (default model update; optional default provider
  update)
    - docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md (config snippet update and LLM call
      shape note)
    - apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py (optional)
    - apps/api/infrastructure/__init__.py (optional export)

    Implementation details

    1. Adapter interface and response container

    - Define in apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py:
        - @dataclass LLMResponse: content: str, provider: str, model: str,
  usage:
          dict | None, raw: dict | None (optional raw for debugging).
        - class LLMAdapter(ABC): async def generate(self, messages: list[dict],
          **kwargs) -> LLMResponse.
    - Keep types local to infrastructure; do not add apps/api/domain/llm.py.

    2. OpenAI Responses API adapter

    - Implement class OpenAIResponsesAdapter(LLMAdapter) with:
        - Constructor: takes api_key, model, optional base_url defaulting to
          https://api.openai.com/v1/responses.
        - generate(...):
            - Validate api_key presence (hard failure: ValueError or custom
              LLMConfigurationError).
            - Build payload:
                - model: from settings (default gpt-5.2).
                - input: list of {role, content} entries (ensure list; raise on
                  malformed input).
                - Optional: temperature, max_output_tokens, metadata if
  provided.
            - Make request via httpx.AsyncClient.post with Authorization:
              Bearer ... and Content-Type: application/json.
            - On non‑200, raise LLMProviderError with response body for
              debuggability.
            - Parse response:
                - Prefer output_text if present and non‑empty.
                - Else extract from output[0].content first item with type ==
                  "output_text" or text.
                - If no content found, raise LLMResponseError.
            - Return LLMResponse(content=..., provider="openai", model=...,
              usage=resp.get("usage")).

    3. Factory function

    - Add get_llm_adapter() that:
        - Reads settings.default_llm_provider.
        - If not "openai", raise NotImplementedError.
        - If openai key missing, hard‑fail.
        - Return cached instance (optional simple module‑level singleton).

    4. Config defaults and spec update

    - Update apps/api/config.py:
        - Set openai_model = "gpt-5.2".
        - Optionally set default_llm_provider = "openai" (or enforce in
  factory).
    - Update docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md:
        - Configuration snippet: openai_model: "gpt-5.2", default_llm_provider:
          "openai".
        - Add a short note in LLM section: “P5.1 uses OpenAI Responses API
          (non‑streaming)”.

    5. Optional unit tests (no network)

    - apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py:
        - Mock httpx.AsyncClient.post to return a known Responses JSON.
        - Assert endpoint URL is /v1/responses.
        - Assert payload uses input and model="gpt-5.2".
        - Assert parser uses output_text and fallback path.
        - Assert missing API key raises hard error.

    Negative patterns to avoid

    - Do NOT use LangChain chat wrappers (ChatOpenAI) or /v1/chat/completions.
    - Do NOT add Anthropic/Google adapters or stubs.
    - Do NOT change apps/api/orchestration/nodes.py or prompts in this slice.
    - Do NOT add streaming.
    - Do NOT relax “hard failure” on missing API key.

    Verification (if tests added)

    - pytest apps/api/tests/unit/test_llm_adapter.py

    Assumptions

    - OpenAI Responses API returns output_text or output[].content[] as per
      current API behavior.
    - httpx is available in apps/api/pyproject.toml (it is).

    Open question (only if you want to pin down)

    - Enforce default_llm_provider == "openai" via settings validation vs
  factory
      check only.

  </Co-Developer-1>

  • Assessment: the Senior Developer plan diverges from the directives and risks
    scope creep for P5.1.

    Key mismatches vs directives

    - Multi‑provider (Anthropic/OpenAI/Google) conflicts with the explicit
      OpenAI‑only + Responses API requirement; Doc 3 also defers multi‑LLM.
    - Use of ChatOpenAI/LangChain chat clients doesn’t guarantee the Responses
  API

    - Modifying apps/api/orchestration/nodes.py and adding prompt routing stubs

      changes are premature.

      hard‑failure requirement.

      conflicts with gpt-5.2 + OpenAI default and the spec snippet update you
      requested.
    - Integration tests with live APIs require network access and keys; not
      appropriate for this slice.

    Best path to achieve the objective

    - Implement a single OpenAI Responses API adapter using httpx.AsyncClient in
      apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py; no LangChain chat wrappers.
    - Add a minimal factory that hard‑fails if settings.openai_api_key is
  missing

    - Update apps/api/config.py default openai_model = "gpt-5.2" and align the
      config snippet in docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md.
    - Keep P5.1 scoped to the adapter only; defer prompt logic and node
      integration to P5.2.
    - Optional unit tests should mock httpx and assert endpoint + payload shape
  +
      parsing; avoid live API tests.

      ---
      My Perspective

      Co-Developer-1's approach is better for P5.1. Here's why:

      1. Correct Slice Granularity

      My plan tried to do too much in P5.1:
      - Adapter + factory + node integration + prompt stubs = 4 concerns
      - Co-Dev-1: Adapter + factory only = focused, testable unit

      The slice protocol says "plan → approval → implement → report." A tighter
    scope means faster feedback loops.

      2. Direct API Control

      Using httpx directly instead of LangChain:
      - Pro: Full control over request shape for Responses API
      - Pro: No abstraction leakage from LangChain
      - Pro: raw field in response aids debugging
      - Con: We have langchain-openai in pyproject.toml already

      However, LangChain's ChatOpenAI targets /v1/chat/completions, not /v1/
    responses. The Responses API is a different endpoint with a different
  payload
    shape (input vs messages). Direct httpx is correct here.

      3. Simpler Interface

      My 3-method interface (check_input_sufficiency, generate_step_content,
    validate_step_content) bakes in domain assumptions. Co-Dev-1's single
    generate(messages) is:
      - More general-purpose
      - Easier to test
      - Domain-specific methods can wrap it in P5.2

      4. Hard Failure on Missing Key

      Both plans agree here. Good.

      5. Model Specification

      User explicitly said gpt-5.2 with OpenAI Responses API. My plan used
    existing defaults. Co-Dev-1 is correct.

      ---
      Concerns / Questions

      1. Spec update scope: Co-Dev-1 proposes updating docs/spec/3_solver-
    technical-spec.md. Is spec modification in scope for a Senior Developer, or
    should this be a separate Architect action?
      2. Config default change: Changing default_llm_provider from "anthropic"
  to
    "openai" and openai_model from "gpt-4o" to "gpt-5.2" affects the whole
    system. Should this be guarded by a DECISIONS.md entry since it diverges
  from
    the original spec defaults?
      3. Future multi-provider: The ABC interface is good, but
    NotImplementedError for non-OpenAI providers means P5.1 doesn't validate the
    abstraction will work for Anthropic/Google later. Acceptable risk for now.

      ---
      My Recommendation

      Adopt Co-Developer-1's plan with these clarifications:

      1. Scope: P5.1 = adapter only. Node integration and prompts move to P5.2.
      2. API: Use httpx directly to /v1/responses (not LangChain).
      3. Interface: Single generate(messages, **kwargs) -> LLMResponse method.
      4. Config: Update openai_model to gpt-5.2, optionally update
    default_llm_provider to openai.
      5. Spec update: Add note about OpenAI Responses API usage in Doc 3.
      6. Tests: Include unit tests with mocked httpx (no network).

✦ Plan: Slice P5.2 (Step 1-3 Prompts)

  Objective: Implement the prompt engineering logic to generate the V1→V2→V3
  methodology documents (Pass 1) and the final artifact packages (Pass 2) for
  Steps 1, 2, and 3, strictly adhering to Doc 1 (Methodology) and Doc 3
  (Schemas).

  Scope:
   - In Scope:
       - Prompt construction for Pass 1 (Methodology V1/V2/V3).
       - Prompt construction for Pass 2 (Execution/Artifact Generation).
       - Integration of OpenAIResponsesAdapter into nodes.py to drive these
         prompts.
       - Refining nodes.py to actually call generate() and handle the response.
   - Out of Scope:
       - Streaming (P5.3/P5.4).
       - Steps 4-10 (stubs only).
       - Complex error recovery (basic retry only).

  Files to Create/Modify:

   1. `apps/api/orchestration/prompts/` (New Modules)
       - common.py: Shared prompt utilities (e.g., system instructions from Doc
         1, schema definitions injected into context).
       - step_1.py: Prompts for Problem Definition (Pass 1 & 2).
       - step_2.py: Prompts for Requirements (Pass 1 & 2).
       - step_3.py: Prompts for Objectives (Pass 1 & 2).

   2. `apps/api/orchestration/nodes.py`
       - Action: Update generate_step_output and check_sufficiency.
       - Logic:
           - Import the appropriate prompt module based on state.current_step.
           - Call get_llm_adapter().generate(messages).
           - Parse result.
           - Update state.

   3. `apps/api/domain/prompts.py` (New - Optional but recommended)
       - Constraint Check: Does Doc 3 allow this? Doc 3 implies domain models
         are pure. Prompts are technically orchestration concerns. Decision:
         Keep prompts in orchestration/prompts/ as Python modules returning
         message lists.

  Detailed Implementation Strategy:

   * Pass 1 Prompts (Methodology):
       - Input: "Create V1 Data Sheet for Step 1...", then "Create V1 To Do
         List...".
       - Context: Ingest Doc 1's definitions of "Data Sheet", "To Do List", etc.
       - Iterative Refinement: "Now create V2 Data Sheet considering the V1
         Detailed Procedure...".
       - Output: The LLM should return the specific markdown or JSON content for
         the document.

   * Pass 2 Prompts (Execution):
       - Input: The V3 Methodology Documents (from Pass 1) + The "Input" (e.g.,
         User Problem for Step 1).
       - Instruction: "Execute the V3 Detailed Procedure. Produce the output
         artifact conforming to [Schema]."
       - Output: A JSON object matching ProblemDefinitionPackage (etc.).

   * The "Responses API" Context:
       - We will leverage the gpt-5.2 model's ability to handle complex
         instructions.
       - The messages payload to the adapter will be constructed as:
   1         [
   2           {"role": "system", "content": "You are SOLVER... [Core Doc 1 
     Instructions]"},
   3           {"role": "user", "content": "[Step specific context and 
     instructions]"}
   4         ]

  Step-by-Step Build:

   1. Create `apps/api/orchestration/prompts/common.py`:
       - Define the "Meta-Prompt" (Doc 1) as a system message constant.
       - Define helper build_messages(system_prompt, user_prompt).

   2. Create `apps/api/orchestration/prompts/step_1.py`:
       - Implement get_pass_1_prompt(sub_step, version, context) -> messages.
       - Implement get_pass_2_prompt(methodology, user_input) -> messages.

   3. Create `apps/api/orchestration/prompts/step_2.py` & `step_3.py`:
       - Similar structure, consuming output of previous steps.

   4. Update `apps/api/orchestration/nodes.py`:
       - Wire up the structure_node (which does the generation) to use these
         prompts.
       - Crucial: Handle the V1->V2->V3 loop for Pass 1. Self-Correction: The
         current nodes.py in P3 might be too simple (linear). We might need a
         mini-loop inside structure_node or expand the graph for Pass 1
         iteration.
       - Refinement: Doc 3 says "Pass 1: Generate methodology documents (V1 ->
         V2 -> V3)". To keep the graph simple, structure_node for Pass 1 can
         handle the internal iteration (call LLM 12 times: 3 versions * 4 docs)
         *OR* we can simplify to just generating the final V3 set if the model
         is capable (unlikely to meet rigor).
       - Decision: structure_node will orchestrate the V1->V3 loop internally
         for Pass 1. It is a "long-running node".

  Verification:
   - pytest apps/api/tests/unit/test_prompts.py: Verify prompt string
     construction (does it contain required Doc 1 keywords?).
   - Manual/Integration test (later): Run a flow and check if artifacts table
     populates with Methodology docs.

  Negative Patterns to Avoid:
   - Hardcoding schemas in strings: Load schemas from
     packages/contracts/*.schema.json if possible, or define them clearly in
     common.py.
   - Ignoring the "4 Documents" rule: Pass 1 must produce DataSheet, ToDoList,
     Guidance, DetailedProcedure.
   - Skipping V1/V2: The logic must explicitly iterate versions.

   Overview

   Implement prompts and LLM generation for Steps 1-3, enabling Pass 1
  (methodology docs: V1→V2→V3) and
   Pass 2 (artifact execution).

   - In scope: Prompt templates, generation engine, node wiring, output parsing,

   A verification

   | File                                     | Purpose
  |
   
  |------------------------------------------|----------------------------------
  ---------|

  Pass 2)
              |

              |
   | apps/api/orchestration/prompts/step_3.py   | Full Step 3 prompts
              |

              |

  check_sufficiency(),

              |
   | apps/api/application/workflow_service.py   | Enhance sync_db_from_state()
  for methodology artifacts
              |

   Implementation Phases

   Phase 1: Prompt Infrastructure

   Create base classes and utilities.

   base.py:
   @dataclass
   class PromptContext:
       workflow_id: str
       instance_number: int
       step_number: int
       pass_type: str  # "definition" or "execution"
       version: Optional[str] = None  # "v1", "v2", "v3"
       document_type: Optional[str] = None
       original_problem: str
       prior_step_output: Optional[dict] = None
       prior_version_docs: Optional[dict] = None  # For V2/V3
       v3_methodology: Optional[dict] = None  # For Pass 2
       human_feedback: Optional[str] = None

   @dataclass
   class PromptTemplate:
       system_prompt: str
       user_prompt: str

       def render(self, context: PromptContext) -> list[dict]:
           """Render to messages list with {{variable}} substitution."""

   Phase 2: Step 1 Prompts

   Implement all 12 methodology templates + execution prompt for Step 1.

   Document order per version: DataSheet → ToDoList → Guidance →
  DetailedProcedure

   Key content per Doc 1:
   - DataSheet: Input/output contract, schemas, validation rules
   - ToDoList: Phased checklist with validation hooks
   - Guidance: Position, principles, anti-patterns, quality criteria
   - DetailedProcedure: State machine, algorithms, decision logic

   V2/V3 pattern: "Considering" prior version docs - read, identify insights,
  incorporate.

   Phase 3: Steps 2-3 Prompts

   Implement prompts for Steps 2 and 3.

   Step 2 (Requirements):
   - Input: Step 1 package (stakeholders, constraints, scope, success criteria)
   - Output: Requirements (FR/NFR/CR/IR) with traceability to Step 1
   - Key IDs: FR-NNN, NFR-NNN, CR-NNN, IR-NNN
   - Coverage map: Step 1 element → requirements

   Step 3 (Objectives):
   - Input: Step 2 package (requirements)
   - Output: Objectives (CAP/QUAL/COMP/INTF) with traceability to Step 2
   - Key IDs: CAP-NNN, QUAL-NNN, COMP-NNN, INTF-NNN
   - Success framework: minimum viable / target / aspirational

   Phase 4: Generation Engine

   generation.py:
   class StepGenerator:
       def __init__(self, adapter: LLMAdapter): ...

       async def generate_methodology(self, step: StepName, context:
  PromptContext) -> dict:
           """Generate V1→V2→V3 for all 4 doc types.
           Returns: {"v1": {...}, "v2": {...}, "v3": {...}}
           """

       async def execute_methodology(self, step: StepName, methodology: dict,
  context: PromptContext) -> 
   dict:
           """Execute V3 methodology to produce step package (JSON mode)."""

   Phase 5: Node Wiring

   nodes.py changes:

   async def generate_step_output(state: WorkflowState) -> dict:
       from orchestration.generation import StepGenerator
       from infrastructure.llm import get_default_adapter

       adapter = get_default_adapter()
       generator = StepGenerator(adapter)
       context = build_prompt_context(state)

       if state.current_pass == PassType.DEFINITION:
           # Pass 1: Generate all 12 methodology docs for this step
           return await generator.generate_methodology(state.current_step,
  context)
       else:
           # Pass 2: Execute V3 methodology to produce package
           methodology = state.methodology.get(state.current_step.value)
           return await generator.execute_methodology(state.current_step,
  methodology, context)

   async def check_sufficiency(state: WorkflowState) -> bool:
       """Use LLM to check if input is sufficient or needs clarification."""
       # Only check sufficiency in Pass 2 Step 1 (initial problem input)
       # Pass 1 is autonomous, Pass 2 Steps 2-3 have prior step output
       ...

   async def validate_output(step: StepName, output: dict) -> ValidationResult:
       """Validate output against Pydantic model (Pass 2) or structure (Pass
  1)."""
       ...

   Phase 6: Output Parsing

   parsing.py:
   - Pydantic models for ProblemDefinitionPackage, RequirementsPackage,
  ObjectivesPackage
   - Match Doc 3 Section 5 schemas exactly
   - Parse JSON from LLM response
   - Validate with clear error messages

   packages.py (domain models):
   class Stakeholder(BaseModel):
       id: str  # SH-NNN
       name_or_group: str
       role: str
       needs: List[str]
       concerns: List[str]
       impact: str

   class ProblemDefinitionPackage(BaseModel):
       package_id: str
       workflow_id: str
       step_number: Literal[1]
       title: str
       canonical_problem_definition: dict
       stakeholders: List[Stakeholder]
       constraints: dict  # {hard: [...], soft: [...]}
       scope: dict  # {in: [...], out: [...]}
       success_criteria: List[dict]
       ...

   Phase 7: Methodology Persistence

   Enhance workflow_service.sync_db_from_state():
   - Detect methodology in state
   - Store each doc as artifact with artifact_type='methodology_doc'
   - Include document_type and document_version fields

   Phase 8: Gate A Verification

   tools/verify_methodology.py:
   python tools/verify_methodology.py --workflow-id <id> --steps 1 2 3
   # Expected: 36 docs
   # Actual: N docs
   # Gate A: PASSED/FAILED

   Key Decisions

   | Decision              | Choice
  | Rationale
                           |
   
  |-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------
  -|------------------------
   ------------------------|
   | Prompt storage        | Constants in Python files
  | Version with code, easy
    to modify              |
   | Methodology structure | {v1: {data_sheet: "...", ...}, v2: {...}, v3:
  {...}} | Matches Doc 1 iteration
    pattern                |
   | Pass 2 output         | JSON mode with Pydantic validation
  | Structured,
   schema-enforced                    |
   | Sufficiency check     | Only Pass 2 Step 1
  | Pass 1 autonomous,
   Steps 2-3 have prior output |

   State Flow

   Pass 1 (DEFINITION):
     Step 1: generate_step_output() → 12 methodology docs →
  state.methodology["problem_definition"]
     Step 2: generate_step_output() → 12 methodology docs →
  state.methodology["requirements"]
     Step 3: generate_step_output() → 12 methodology docs →
  state.methodology["objectives"]
     → 36 docs total → Gate A satisfied

   Pass 2 (EXECUTION):
     Step 1: generate_step_output() → ProblemDefinitionPackage →
  state.artifacts["problem_definition"]
     Step 2: generate_step_output() → RequirementsPackage →
  state.artifacts["requirements"]
     Step 3: generate_step_output() → ObjectivesPackage →
  state.artifacts["objectives"]
     → 3 packages → Gate B (partial)

   Verification Commands

   # Gate A

   Risks & Mitigations

   | Risk                     | Mitigation
  |
   
  |--------------------------|--------------------------------------------------
  ------------|
   | LLM output quality       | Explicit structure in prompts, examples, retry
  with feedback |
   | Token limits             | Monitor usage, chunk context if needed
  |
   | Parse failures           | JSON mode, Pydantic validation, max 3 retries
  |
   | Checkpoint serialization | Ensure methodology dict is JSON-serializable
  |

   Constraints Honored

   - Doc 1 methodology (V1→V2→V3, 4 docs per step)
   - Pass 1 autonomous (gate policy controls interrupts)
   - Pass 2 human-gated
   - Flat layout (apps/api/)
   - Use LLM adapter from P5.1
   - Traceability IDs (SH-, FR-, CAP-*)

│ ✓  Shell cat /Users/ryan/ai-env/projects/solver-engine/docs/Working/Archive… │
│                                                                              │
│ ... first 371 lines hidden ...                                               │
│ **Instance Relationship:** In Instance 0 (universal), this methodology is    │
│ domain-agnostic. In Instance N, it will be specialized with domain-specific  │
│ stakeholder types, constraint categories, and scope patterns.                │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Core Principles                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 1. Stated vs. Actual Problem                                            │
│ The output explicitly distinguishes:                                         │
│ - **Stated Problem:** What the user literally said (preserved verbatim)      │
│ - **Core Problem:** What actually needs to be solved (derived through        │
│ analysis)                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ These may be identical, or they may differ significantly. Common             │
│ divergences:                                                                 │
│ - Stated problem is a symptom, actual problem is root cause                  │
│ - Stated problem is a solution request, actual problem is the need behind it │
│ - Stated problem is partial, actual problem is more complete                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Always capture both. Document any difference and explain why.                │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 2. Elicitation Discipline                                               │
│ - **Never proceed with critical gaps.** A critical gap is one where          │
│ proceeding would require assumptions that, if wrong, invalidate downstream   │
│ work.                                                                        │
│ - **Ask specific questions.** "Can you clarify X?" is better than "Tell me   │
│ more."                                                                       │
│ - **Batch when possible.** Multiple questions in one interaction is          │
│ preferable to multiple round-trips.                                          │
│ - **Respect the human's time.** Ask only what is necessary.                  │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 3. Stakeholder Completeness                                             │
│ Stakeholders are not just users. Consider:                                   │
│ - **Direct users:** Those who interact with the solution                     │
│ - **Indirect users:** Those affected by the solution's outputs               │
│ - **Operators:** Those who maintain or administer the solution               │
│ - **Beneficiaries:** Those who benefit without direct interaction            │
│ - **Affected parties:** Those impacted even if not benefiting                │
│                                                                              │
│ Cardinality [3,6] ensures meaningful analysis without overwhelming detail.   │
│ If more exist, group or prioritize.                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 4. Constraint Integrity                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ **Hard Constraint Test:** "If this constraint is violated, does the solution │
│ fail entirely?"                                                              │
│ - Yes → Hard constraint (non-negotiable)                                     │
│ - No, but it would be problematic → Soft constraint (preference)             │
│ - It depends → Clarify conditions; may split into hard and soft              │
│                                                                              │
│ **Violation Consequence:** Every hard constraint must have an explicit       │
│ violation consequence. This forces rigor — if you can't state what fails, it │
│ may not be truly hard.                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 5. Scope as Contract                                                    │
│ - **In Scope:** Explicit commitments. The solution WILL address these.       │
│ - **Out of Scope:** Explicit exclusions. The solution will NOT address       │
│ these.                                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ Both are required. Scope by inclusion alone leads to creep. Scope by         │
│ exclusion alone leads to gaps.                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ #### 6. Traceability as First-Class Concern                                  │
│ Every output element must trace to a source:                                 │
│ - **Quoted:** Directly from input                                            │
│ - **Paraphrased:** Restated from input                                       │
│ - **Inferred:** Derived from input (must document inference basis)           │
│                                                                              │
│ No orphan outputs. No fabrication. No assumption presented as fact.          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### State Machine Discipline                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ The procedure is a state machine with explicit states and transitions. Key   │
│ discipline:                                                                  │
│ - **No skipping:** Every phase executes (even if trivially)                  │
│ - **Backward transitions allowed:** For elicitation (ELICITING → ANALYZING)  │
│ and remediation (VALIDATING → STRUCTURING)                                   │
│ - **Human gates are synchronous:** ELICITING and REVIEWING require human     │
│ response before proceeding                                                   │
│ - **Terminal state is explicit:** COMPLETE means approved and ready for Step │
│ 2                                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Anti-Patterns                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│ | Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |                                          │
│ |--------------|---------|--------|                                          │
│ | Conflating stated/actual | No difference_explanation when problems differ  │
│ | Always check; document differences |                                       │
│ | Proceeding with gaps | Assumptions in output | Elicit until critical gaps  │
│ resolved |                                                                   │
│ | Stakeholder omission | Missing affected parties | Systematically check all │
│ categories |                                                                 │
│ | Soft-as-hard | Constraint without clear violation consequence | Apply hard │
│ constraint test rigorously |                                                 │
│ | Unbounded scope | IN scope only, no OUT scope | Require both explicitly |  │
│ | Orphan outputs | Elements without source | Traceability mapping before     │
│ validation |                                                                 │
│ | Skipping validation | Errors propagate | Execute all validation rules |    │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Quality Criteria for Step 1 Output                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ The output is ready for Step 2 when:                                         │
│ 1. Core problem is singular, focused, and ≤ 2 sentences                      │
│ 2. 3-6 stakeholders identified with complete attributes                      │
│ 3. 3-6 hard constraints with violation consequences                          │
│ 4. Soft constraints have trade-offs documented                               │
│ 5. Scope has both IN and OUT explicitly defined                              │
│ 6. Success criteria are verifiable and trace to stakeholders/constraints     │
│ 7. Every output element has traceability                                     │
│ 8. Human has explicitly approved                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ ---                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Detailed Procedure V3                                                     │
│ *Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3*          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### State Machine Specification                                              │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING,           │
│ REVIEWING, COMPLETE}                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ INITIAL_STATE = RECEIVED                                                     │
│ TERMINAL_STATE = COMPLETE                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ TRANSITIONS:                                                                 │
│   RECEIVED    → ANALYZING   : on begin_processing()                          │
│   ANALYZING   → ELICITING   : on critical_gaps_exist()                       │
│   ANALYZING   → STRUCTURING : on no_critical_gaps()                          │
│   ELICITING   → ANALYZING   : on clarification_received()                    │
│   STRUCTURING → VALIDATING  : on extraction_complete()                       │
│   VALIDATING  → STRUCTURING : on validation_failure()                        │
│   VALIDATING  → REVIEWING   : on validation_success()                        │
│   REVIEWING   → STRUCTURING : on rejection(feedback)                         │
│   REVIEWING   → COMPLETE    : on approval()                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ HUMAN_GATES = {ELICITING, REVIEWING}                                         │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Phase 1: Reception                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ **State:** RECEIVED                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ **Input:**                                                                   │
│ - raw_problem_description: string (from human)                               │
│                                                                              │
│ **Procedure:**                                                               │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ 1. LOG input with timestamp                                                  │
│ 2. INITIALIZE Step1Output:                                                   │
│    - metadata.instance = current_instance                                    │
│    - metadata.step = 1                                                       │
│    - metadata.state = "RECEIVED"                                             │
│    - metadata.timestamp = now()                                              │
│    - all other fields = null                                                 │
│ 3. ACKNOWLEDGE receipt to human (optional, context-dependent)                │
│ 4. TRANSITION to ANALYZING                                                   │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ **Output:**                                                                  │
│ - Logged input                                                               │
│ - Initialized Step1Output structure                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ **Validation Gate:** Input logged, structure initialized                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ---                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Phase 2: Comprehension & Gap Analysis                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ **State:** ANALYZING                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ **Input:**                                                                   │
│ - raw_problem_description (original)                                         │
│ - clarifying_responses (if any, from prior ELICITING)                        │
│                                                                              │
│ **Procedure:**                                                               │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ 1. PARSE input corpus for explicit elements:                                 │
│                                                                              │
│    a. EXTRACT stated_problem:                                                │
│       - Locate the core request/problem sentence(s)                          │
│       - Record verbatim quote                                                │
│       - Record source reference                                              │
│                                                                              │
│    b. IDENTIFY mentioned stakeholders:                                       │
│       - List all parties explicitly named or referenced                      │
│       - Note their stated roles or relationships                             │
│                                                                              │
│    c. IDENTIFY stated constraints:                                           │
│       - List all limitations, restrictions, requirements mentioned           │
│       - Preserve original phrasing                                           │
│                                                                              │
│    d. IDENTIFY implied scope:                                                │
│       - What does the problem suggest is included?                           │
│       - What does it suggest is excluded?                                    │
│                                                                              │
│    e. IDENTIFY stated success criteria:                                      │
│       - Any explicit statements about what success looks like                │
│                                                                              │
│ 2. ANALYZE for gaps:                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│    a. AMBIGUITIES:                                                           │
│       - Phrases with multiple interpretations                                │
│       - Undefined terms                                                      │
│       - Vague qualifiers ("fast", "easy", "good")                            │
│                                                                              │
│    b. MISSING INFORMATION:                                                   │
│       - Required fields per Data Sheet with no source                        │
│       - Stakeholder categories with no representation                        │
│       - Success criteria not mentioned                                       │
│                                                                              │
│    c. CONTRADICTIONS:                                                        │
│       - Statements that conflict                                             │
│       - Constraints that are mutually exclusive                              │
│                                                                              │
│ 3. CLASSIFY each gap:                                                        │
│    - CRITICAL: Proceeding would require assumption that, if wrong,           │
│ invalidates work                                                             │
│    - MINOR: Can be reasonably inferred with stated basis                     │
│                                                                              │
│ 4. DECISION:                                                                 │
│    - IF critical_gaps.count > 0:                                             │
│        TRANSITION to ELICITING                                               │
│    - ELSE:                                                                   │
│        TRANSITION to STRUCTURING                                             │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ **Output:**                                                                  │
│ - Comprehension summary                                                      │
│ - Gap list with classifications                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ **Validation Gate:** Comprehension summary complete; gap list complete       │
│                                                                              │
│ ---                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ### Phase 3: Elicitation                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ **State:** ELICITING                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ **Human Interaction:** Required (synchronous)                                │
│                                                                              │
│ **Input:**                                                                   │
│ - gap_list (critical gaps only)                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ **Procedure:**                                                               │
│ ```                                                                          │
│ 1. FORMULATE clarifying questions:                                           │
│    FOR EACH critical_gap IN gap_list:                                        │

  │
       │ - V1 ToDoList: Considering Data Sheet V1
  │
       │ - V1 Guidance: Considering Seed Problem + Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1
  │
       │ - V1 DetailedProcedure: Considering Data Sheet V1 + To Do List V1 +
  Guidance V1                   │
       │ - V2 DataSheet: Considering Detailed Procedure V1 + Guidance V1 + To Do
  List V1                   │
       │ - V2 ToDoList: Considering Data Sheet V2 + Guidance V1 + Detailed
  Procedure V1                    │
       │ - V2 Guidance: Considering Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 + Detailed
  Procedure V1                  │
       │ - V2 DetailedProcedure: Considering Data Sheet V2 + To Do List V2 +
  Guidance V2                   │
       │ - V3 follows same pattern considering V2 docs
  │
       │
  │
       │ Key content per document type:
  │
       │ - DataSheet: Input/output specs (YAML), validation rules table, state
  definitions table           │
       │ - ToDoList: Checklist with - [ ] items, phase headers, Validation:
  hooks                          │
       │ - Guidance: Principles, anti-patterns table, quality criteria list
  │
       │ - DetailedProcedure: State machine spec, phase procedures with
  pseudocode                         │
       │
  │
       │ Phase 3: Steps 2-3 Prompts
  │
       │
  │
       │ Implement prompts for Steps 2 and 3.
  │
       │
  │
       │ Step 2 Key Differences (from reference session):
  │
       │
  │
       │ 1. Input Validation Gate (in Reception phase):
  │
       │ VALIDATE:
  │
       │   - Step 1 state = COMPLETE
  │
       │   - Human approval present
  │
       │   - All required fields present
  │
       │
  │
       │ 2. Derivation Patterns Table (in Data Sheet):
  │
       │ | Source Type | Derivation Pattern | Category | Priority |
  │
       │ |-------------|-------------------|----------|----------|
  │
       │ | Core Problem | Problem → Capability → FR | FR | must |
  │
       │ | Stakeholder Need | Need → What satisfies it | FR/NFR | must/should |
  │
       │ | Stakeholder Concern | Concern → What mitigates it | NFR | should |
  │
       │ | Hard Constraint | Constraint → CR directly | CR | must |
  │
       │ | Soft Constraint | Constraint → FR/NFR | FR/NFR | should/could |
  │
       │ | Success Criterion | Criterion → Verification need | FR/NFR |
  must/should |                      │
       │
  │
       │ 3. Coverage Mapping (in Output):
  │
       │ coverage:
  │
       │   stakeholders: mapping of SH-XXX → [FR-001, FR-002, ...]
  │
       │   constraints: mapping of HC-XXX → [CR-001, ...]
  │
       │   problem: [requirement_ids addressing core problem]
  │
       │
  │

  │

  │

  HC-, SC-*                  │

  │
       │ - Key IDs: CAP-NNN, QUAL-NNN, COMP-NNN, INTF-NNN
  │
       │ - Transformation: FR→CAP, NFR→QUAL, CR→COMP
  │
       │ - Success framework: minimum viable / target / aspirational tiers
  │

  <Co-Developer-1>
  Assessment
    The Senior Developer’s plan shows strong understanding of the reference
    session and the iterative V1→V2→V3 structure, but it still overshoots P5.2
  and
    diverges from the directives I gave earlier.

    Where it aligns

    - Correctly captures the V1→V2→V3 cadence and the four doc types.
    - Uses the reference session to structure prompts (good).
    - Limits sufficiency checks to Pass 2 Step 1 (consistent with Doc 1 and our
      guidance).
    - Keeps Pass 2 outputs JSON‑first (good for schema alignment).

    Where it deviates from the directives

    - Scope creep: adds methodology persistence
      (workflow_service.sync_db_from_state) and Gate A tooling (tools/
      verify_methodology.py) which are P5.3/P6.1 scope, not P5.2.
    - New domain module apps/api/domain/packages.py with Pydantic models
  violates
      the “domain is pure, no external dependencies” rule; also not in Doc 3.
    - LLM adapter changes (JSON mode support) are unnecessary for P5.2 and risk
      undermining P5.1’s adapter contract.
    - Prompt infrastructure explosion: base classes and generation engine are
  fine
      if kept minimal, but this plan introduces multiple new layers and
  artifacts
      beyond what’s needed to deliver prompts.

    Best path to achieve the objective

    - Keep P5.2 strictly about prompts + orchestration usage:
        - Build prompt templates for Steps 1–3 (Pass 1 V1→V2→V3, Pass 2
          execution).
        - Add minimal wiring in nodes.py to call prompts through the P5.1
  adapter.
        - Parse LLM output only to the extent needed for the node to return a
          dict; avoid full Pydantic models in domain/.
    - Defer methodology persistence, schema validation tooling, and Gate A
  scripts
      to P5.3/P6.1.
    - Use reference session as exemplars, but force Pass 2 to emit strict JSON
  per
      Doc 3 schemas (no narrative output, no “seed IDs”).

    Perspective given the reference session
    The session demonstrates that everything can be produced “one prompt at a
    time,” which is exactly why the prompt templates should be simple and
    explicit, not embedded in a heavy generation engine. The best outcome is to
    codify those exemplar structures directly into prompt templates, then
  iterate
    via V1→V2→V3 calls without inventing new layers.

    If you want, I can restate the trimmed P5.2 plan in a strict,
    slice‑appropriate format that stays within scope.

✦ I have evaluated Co-Developer-1's critique.

  Evaluation:

  Co-Developer-1 correctly identifies scope creep in the Senior Developer's
  plan.
   1. Persistence: Implementing sync_db_from_state logic for methodology docs
      belongs in P5.3 (Artifact Storage), not P5.2 (Prompts).
   2. Verification Tool: verify_methodology.py is explicitly P6.1 (Gate A Test).
   3. Domain Models: Adding apps/api/domain/packages.py is unnecessary overhead
      now. We can use raw dicts or simple TypedDicts for P5.2, or just validate
      structure in nodes.py.
   4. JSON Mode: While useful, P5.1 established the adapter contract. If we need
      JSON mode, we should pass it as a parameter to generate() if supported, or
      rely on prompt engineering ("Return ONLY JSON") for this slice to keep the
      adapter stable. Correction: Doc 3 schemas are strict, so JSON enforcement
      is critical. However, Co-Dev-1 is right that modifying the infrastructure
      adapter itself (P5.1 scope) in P5.2 is a risk. We should try to achieve
      this via prompts first or minimal kwargs.

  Perspective:
  The core objective of P5.2 is Prompts. The logic to store the result or verify
  the result across the whole system belongs in subsequent slices. We must keep
  the slice focused on:
   - Writing the prompt strings/templates.
   - Wiring them to the LLM.
   - Getting a response.

  Reflected Decision:
  I agree with Co-Developer-1. We must trim the plan to focusing strictly on
  Prompt Construction and Execution. Storage and Verification are next.

  Revised Plan (P5.2 - Prompts Only):
   1. Prompts: Implement
      apps/api/orchestration/prompts/{base,common,step_1,step_2,step_3}.py.
   2. Generation: Implement StepGenerator in generation.py to handle the V1->V3
      loop logic (since this logic is part of how the prompts are used).
   3. Wiring: Update nodes.py to use StepGenerator.
   4. Parsing: Minimal parsing to ensure we get a dict back.
   5. Exclusions: No database persistence changes. No verify_methodology.py
      script. No new domain/ models (use dicts for now).

   P5.2 Implementation Plan: Step 1-3 Prompts (Scope-Tight)

   Overview

   Implement prompt templates for Steps 1-3 (Pass 1 methodology + Pass 2
  execution) using exemplars from
   claude-devsession-alpha.txt. Minimal node wiring to invoke prompts via P5.1
  adapter.

   Scope (Strict)

   - In scope:
     - Prompt templates for Steps 1-3 (Pass 1 + Pass 2)
     - Minimal orchestration in nodes.py to invoke prompts

   - Out of scope (deferred):

     - Traceability persistence → P5.4

   Document Format (from Reference Session)

   The actual methodology documents follow this concrete format:

   Data Sheet V3 Format

   Considering: Detailed Procedure V2 + Guidance Document V2 + To Do List V2

   ### Input Specification
   | Field | Type | Required | Description |
   |-------|------|----------|-------------|
   | raw_problem_description | string | Yes | Initial input from human |
   | clarifying_responses | list[QA_pair] | Conditional | Responses to
  elicitation |

   ### Output Specification (YAML)
   Step1Output:
     stakeholders:        # Cardinality: [3,6]
       - id: string       # SH-001 format
         name: string
         role: string
         primary_need: string
     constraints:
       hard:              # Cardinality: [3,6]
         - id: string     # HC-001 format
           statement: string
           violation_consequence: string
       soft:
         - id: string     # SC-001 format

   ### Validation Rules
   | Rule ID | Field | Rule | Severity |
   |---------|-------|------|----------|
   | V1 | core_problem.statement | Length ≤ 2 sentences | Error |
   | V2 | stakeholders | Count ∈ [3,6] | Error |

   ### State Definitions
   | State | Entry Condition | Valid Transitions |
   |-------|-----------------|-------------------|
   | RECEIVED | Input logged | → ANALYZING |
   | ANALYZING | Begin processing | → ELICITING, → STRUCTURING |

   To Do List V3 Format

   Considering: Data Sheet V3 + Guidance Document V2 + Detailed Procedure V2

   #### 1. Reception [RECEIVED → ANALYZING]
   - [ ] Log raw_problem_description with timestamp
   - [ ] Initialize output structure with metadata
   - [ ] Set state: ANALYZING
   - **Validation:** Input logged, structure initialized

   #### 2. Stakeholder Analysis [STRUCTURING]
   - [ ] For each stakeholder (3-6):
     - [ ] Assign id (SH-XXX)
     - [ ] Define name, role
     - [ ] Mark source (quoted|paraphrased|inferred)
   - **Validation:** V2 (count), V3 (inference basis)

   Guidance Document V3 Format

   Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Detailed Procedure V2

   ### Purpose
   [Orient the agent to context and principles]

   ### Context
   **Position in Workflow:** Step 1 is the foundation...

   ### Core Principles
   #### 1. Stated vs. Actual Problem
   - **Stated Problem:** What the user literally said
   - **Core Problem:** What actually needs to be solved

   ### Anti-Patterns
   | Anti-Pattern | Symptom | Remedy |
   |--------------|---------|--------|
   | Conflating stated/actual | No difference_explanation | Always check |

   ### Quality Criteria for Step 1 Output
   1. Core problem is singular, focused, ≤ 2 sentences
   2. 3-6 stakeholders with complete attributes

   Detailed Procedure V3 Format

   Considering: Data Sheet V3 + To Do List V3 + Guidance Document V3

   ### State Machine Specification
   STATES = {RECEIVED, ANALYZING, ELICITING, STRUCTURING, VALIDATING, REVIEWING,
  COMPLETE}
   TRANSITIONS:
     RECEIVED    → ANALYZING   : on begin_processing()
     ANALYZING   → ELICITING   : on critical_gaps_exist()
     ANALYZING   → STRUCTURING : on no_critical_gaps()

   ### Phase 1: Reception
   **State:** RECEIVED
   **Input:** raw_problem_description
   **Procedure:**
   1. LOG input with timestamp
   2. INITIALIZE Step1Output structure
   3. TRANSITION to ANALYZING
   **Validation Gate:** Input logged, structure initialized

   Implementation Phases

   Phase 1: Common Helpers (common.py)

   Minimal utilities only:
   def build_context(state: WorkflowState, prior_docs: dict, current_docs: dict)
  -> dict:
       """Build context dict for prompt formatting."""
       return {
           "instance_number": state.instance_number,
           "step_name": state.current_step.value,
           "original_problem": state.original_problem,
           "prior_step_output":
  state.artifacts.get(prev_step(state.current_step)),
           "prior_version_docs": prior_docs,
           "current_version_docs": current_docs,
           "v3_methodology": state.methodology.get(state.current_step.value,
  {}).get("v3"),
           "human_feedback": state.step_state.human_feedback,
       }

   def parse_json_output(content: str) -> dict:
       """Parse JSON from LLM response with basic error handling."""
       # Strip markdown code fences if present
       # Parse with json.loads
       # Return dict or raise with clear error

   def considering_header(doc_type: str, version: str, refs: list[str]) -> str:
       """Generate 'Considering: X + Y + Z' header line."""

   Phase 2: Step 1 Prompts (step_1.py)

   12 Pass 1 prompts following the "Considering:" pattern:

   | Version | Document          | Considers
  |
   
  |---------|-------------------|-----------------------------------------------
  ----|
   | V1      | DataSheet         | Seed problem
